      ]
    },

      "id": "Asal mula nama bunyip ditelusuri dari bahasa Wemba-Wemba atau Wergaia orang Aboriginal di Victoria, yang kini kerap diterjemahkan sebagai devil atau evil spirit [bunyip-c02] [bunyip-c03].",
      "en": "Linguistic roots trace to the Wemba-Wemba and Wergaia languages of Victoria, with modern Aboriginal Australians often translating the term as devil or evil spirit [bunyip-c02] [bunyip-c03].",

      "source_id": "ukupanipo-s1",

      "quote": "Taotao Mo'na, also commonly written as taotaomona or taotaomo'na (Chamoru taotao, \"person/people\" and mo'na \"precede\", loosely translated as \"people before history\" or \"ancient people\"), are spirits of ancient giant inhabitants believed to protect the mountains and wild places of the Mariana Islands, which include Luta, Saipan, Tinian and Guam, in Micronesia.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taotao Mo'na adalah roh penduduk raksasa purba penjaga pegunungan dan tempat liar di Kepulauan Mariana.",
        "en": "Taotao Mo'na are spirits of ancient giant inhabitants protecting mountains in the Mariana Islands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taotao-mona-c02",
      "source_id": "taotao-mona-s1",
      "quote": "Locals and \"traditional\" Chamorros claim that one must request permission from the Taotao Mo'na before entering the jungle or taking fruit or wood from it.",
      "locator": "Beliefs",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warga meyakini perlunya memohon izin sebelum memasuki rimba atau mengambil buah dan kayu.",
        "en": "Locals believe permission must be requested before entering the jungle or harvesting fruit and wood."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "cultural_context",
      "searched": "Pengaruh kolonialisasi Spanyol terhadap pemujaan arwah leluhur di Guam."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Batu latte dan peninggalan megalitikum berstatus cagar budaya terlindungi."
    }
  ]
}

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Māori suku Ngāti Hau yang dicatat oleh John White, Hāhau-whenua adalah nama ikan agung yang dipancing oleh Māui dan menjelma menjadi North Island di New Zealand [hahau-whenua-c01].",
      "en": "In Māori Ngāti Hau tradition recorded by John White, Hāhau-whenua is the name of the colossal fish hooked by Māui that became the North Island of New Zealand [hahau-whenua-c01].",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hahau-whenua-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika Māui mengangkat hasil tangkapannya ke permukaan samudra, ia mendapati bahwa ikan yang terpancing itu adalah Hāhau-whenua [hahau-whenua-c02].",
      "en": "Upon hauling the hooked creature upward from the depths, Māui recognized the great fish to be Hāhau-whenua [hahau-whenua-c02].",

        "hahau-whenua-c02"
      ]

      "en": "In Māori mythology, the hākuturi are recognized as dedicated guardians of the sacred forest [hakuturi-c01].",
